Part III Materials

===== [p238-238] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Conclusion (5块) =====
--[p238]-- Other materials are required to create microstructures having low electrical resistivity, high mechanical toughness, high refractive index, optoelectronic function, or resilience under extreme temperature, to give but a few examples. This has led investigators to develop innovative photopolymer composites or nonpolymeric photo-responsive media that can be used to directly create structures with the required properties.
--[p238]-- Creative and complementary methods of post-MPL processing have also been reported – including surface functionalization, templated deposition, and backfilling – which can be used to direct one or more materials with the desired properties into a targeted microscale form defined by MPL using typical photopolymers.
More such developments can be expected as the technology of MPL matures and as interest in its use grows, particularly for industrial manufacturing.
--[p238]-- Areas for fruitful exploration include developing methods for patterning multifunctional materials, “smart” materials that actively respond to external stimuli, and active-form materials that cause the shape of the microstructure itself to change in response to environmental conditions.
--[p238]-- The existing and emerging material systems for MPL can be expected to form the foundation for dramatic advances in the fields of micro and nanophotonics, opto-fluidics, MEMS, optomechanical systems, sensors, and biomedical devices.
--[p238]-- Conclusion
The growing need for functional microstructured materials and devices has driven the rapid development of new processes for MPL. Three-dimensional microfabrication using photopolymers has led to exciting developments and applications. Yet conventional photopolymers alone cannot provide many of the physical and chemical properties that are essential for existing applications, let alone future needs.

===== [p89-89] Light Sources and Systems for Multiphoton Lithography (3块) =====
--[p89]-- In addition to these components, an MPP setup usually includes other elements: some ofthem support the user in sample processing (e.g., various sample illumination techniques, real-time process observation, focus finder), and other elements allow selective process control or optimization (e.g., spatial light modulators (SLMs) or difractive optical elements (DOEs) can be used for parallel processing).
--[p89]-- In this chapter, we discuss laser light sources and systems for multiphoton lithography from the view points of the underlying concept, characteristics, and performance.
--[p89]-- Light Sources and Systems for Multiphoton Lithography
UlfHinze and Boris Chichkov
A setup for multi photon polymerization (MPP) comprises at least three key components:
1) light source
2) focusing optics
3) nano-positioning or beam-deflection unit.
The technical characteristics of these key components very much define the performance of an MPP setup.

===== [p281-281] Fabricating Nano and Microstructures Made by Narrow Bandgap Semiconductors and Metals using Multiphoton Lithography > Fabrication of 3D Structures Made by PbSe with Multiphoton Lithography (3块) =====
--[p281]-- Fabrication of 3D Structures Made by PbSe with Multiphoton Lithography
As a typical narrow electronic bandgap semiconductor, bulk rock-salt PbSe has a bandgap as narrow as 0.27 eV. It has a refractive index as high as 4.82 at 6 μm [13], which is the largest refractive index value at 6 μm among the current materials used in the multiphoton lithography technique.
--[p281]-- The third-order nonlinear refractive index of PbSe is $1 0 ^ { - 1 3 } \mathrm { c m } ^ { 2 } \mathrm { W } ^ { - 1 }$ [16], which is about one order of magnitude larger than that of ${ \mathrm { A s } } _ { 2 } { \mathrm { S } } _ { 3 }$ chalcogenide glass [17].
--[p281]-- In addition, PbSe exhibits unique optical and electrical properties such as multi-exciton generation [18], high carrier mobility, and the unique thermoelectric property with a thermoelectric figure of merit (ZT) value larger than 1 [19], which makes it a highly promising material applicable in photonics, nanoelectronics, and nano-thermoelectrics.

--[p51]-- The last dip at 309 nm corresponds to a position nearly twice the energy of the main $\mathrm { S } _ { 0 } \to \mathrm { S } _ { 1 }$ absorption band. As a result, the 2PA cross section at this energy (corresponding excitation wavelength is 618 nm) cannot be experimentally determined because of linear absorption.
--[p51]-- 2.4 Examples of Use of Multiple Techniques57
intense band at $\lambda = 4 0 8$ nm with $\delta _ { \mathrm { 2 P A } } = 7 6 0 \mathrm { G M } ;$ and a much more intense band at $\lambda = 3 5 0 \mathrm { n m }$ with $\delta _ { \mathrm { 2 P A } } = 5 2 0 0 \mathrm { G M }$ . ===== [p238-238] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Structures Fabricated in ChG–NP Composites (3块) =====
--[p238]-- Structures Fabricated in ChG–NP Composites
${ \mathrm { A s } } _ { 2 } { \mathrm { S } } _ { 3 }$ was doped with erbium (Er) to fabricate microstructures that possess both high refractive index and room-temperature photoluminescence (PL). Er provides PL at 1.5 μm. Utilizing the gas-phase direct doping (GPDD) technique, Er can be co-evaporated with ${ \mathrm { A s } } _ { 2 } { \mathrm { S } } _ { 3 }$ forming Er-doped ${ \mathrm { A s } } _ { 2 } { \mathrm { S } } _ { 3 }$ thin films.
--[p238]-- Wong et al. fabricated high-quality 3D PCs via MPL in the $\mathrm { E r / A s _ { 2 } S _ { 3 } }$ photoresist with minimum features sizes of 180 nm and PL at 1.5 μm [147, 148]. Preliminary studies by Nicoletti et al. suggest that PCs fabricated in ${ \mathrm { A s } } _ { 2 } { \mathrm { S } } _ { 3 }$ can be functionalized by coating with PbSe/CdSe core–shell NPs [153].
--[p238]-- Diaminoheptane was used to create a selfassembled monolayer of functional ligands, which act as selective linkers so that NPs can be added to the ChG PC in a continuous surface layer. The lattice constant of the PC was chosen to overlap with the NIR emission of the NPs to control their spontaneous emission [153].

===== [p123-124] Photoinitiators for Multiphoton Absorption Lithography > 6.1.2.1 History of the Design of Two-Photon Initiators (4块) =====
--[p123]-- On the other hand, there is now a strong demand for eficient TPA dyes for a wide range of applications, including microscopy, microfabrication, 3D data storage, optical power limiting, upconverted lasing, and photodynamic therapy, as well as for the localized release of bioactive species [9]. Recently, this demand has been matched by rapid advances in the design and synthesis of TPA dyes.
--[p124]-- The cross section of these one-photon initiators is quite low – but their radical generation eficiency is high. They are applied in SCR500 and SU-8 photoresists [12].
--[p123]-- 6.1.2.1 History of the Design of Two-Photon Initiators
TPA has become easier to investigate with the development of subpicosecond pulsed lasers since the 1990s (particularly the Ti:sapphire laser). The invention of two-photon excited fluorescence microscopy (TPEF) by Webb and coworkers and the rapid assimilation of this technique by manufacturers of confocal microscopes have led to an explosion of interest in the investigation of various types of two-photon chromophores [8, 9].
--[p124]-- Based on the understanding of TPA dyes, two-photon initiators (TPIs) has emerged, and two-photon polymerization has become a new research field [3]. Numerous studies on the design and synthesis of TPIs have been carried out. The classification and the application of TPIs are discussed in the following sections.
The TPIs investigated in the initial stage were conventional one-photon initiators, such as Irgacure 369 and Irgacure 184 [10, 11].

===== [p230-230] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Multiphoton Processing of Semiconductor Composites and Materials (2块) =====
--[p230]-- Multiphoton Processing of Semiconductor Composites and Materials
Semiconductor devices are important for electronic, optical, and sensing applications. Significant strides have been achieved in extending MPL for patterning semiconductors through the development of specialized photo-patternable chalcogenides, polymer composites that contain semiconductor NPs or their chemical precursors, and post-patterning processes, such as ALD.
--[p230]-- Understandably, many investigations have focused on demonstrating new material processes by creating functional 3D PCs, waveguides, or other optical devices, for which semiconductor materials are often best suited.

===== [p293-295] Fabricating Nano and Microstructures Made by Narrow Bandgap Semiconductors and Metals using Multiphoton Lithography > Conclusions (5块) =====
--[p295]-- The demonstration of 3D nano and microstructures made of PbSe and silver enables the implementation of materials with unique intrinsic properties for 3D device applications, which are otherwise unavailable for wide electronic bandgap materials. With a similar lithography principle, PbSe and silver used for fabrication can be extended to other kinds of narrow bandgap semiconductors and metals (such as lead telluride, cadmium sulfide, cadmium selenide, and gold).
--[p293]-- Reproduced with permission of American Institute of Physics.)
development of multiphoton lithography technique based on the bottom-up strategy is still at its infancy, especially for the fabrication of nano and microstructures made of narrow electronic bandgap semiconductors.
--[p295]-- We expect that this approach can provide a simple and feasible nanofabrication technique based on far-field optics for producing nanophotonic and nanoelectronic devices made from a wide range of unique materials including narrow electronic bandgap semiconductors and metals.
--[p293]-- The arrow indicates the direction of the increase of the exposure time from 5 to 50 ms and the laser power from 0.05 to 0.4 mW. (b) Feature size of the silver dots plotted as a function of the exposure time and the laser power.
(c) Left: SEM image of the silver dot fabricated with the exposure time of 5 ms under the exposure of the laser beam of 0.05 mW. The scale bar is 50 nm. Right: Graph for the plot of the cross-sectional profile along the blue line in left SEM image. (Cao 2013 [14].
--[p293]-- Conclusions
In this chapter, we introduced the progress in the fabrication of nano and microstructures made of narrow electronic bandgap semiconductors and metals via multiphoton lithography. Compared with the top-down strategy, the current

===== [p219-219] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Multiphoton Processing of Metallic Composites and Materials (3块) =====
--[p219]-- These include physical and chemical deposition of metal after MPL, coating functionalized surfaces with metal NPs after fabrication, and direct fabrication of metal-containing structures using composites that include components of the targeted metal. The simplest way to create a metallized 3D structure is to fabricate it by MPL using a conventional polymeric resin and then deposit the targeted metal physically or chemically.
--[p219]-- Multiphoton Processing of Metallic Composites and Materials
Metals can be used to create structures having high electrical and thermal conductivity, high mechanical strength, good optical reflectivity, and other novel properties. Several approaches have been developed for creating metallized structures in conjunction with MPL.
--[p219]-- This can be achieved through CVD or by physical metal vapor deposition, where the metal is vaporized by resistive heating, electron beam (e-beam) evaporation, or magnetron sputtering (MS).
Electrochemical methods can also be used to deposit metal onto the surface after fabrication. Metal structures can be written directly using photo-patternable composites that contain a source of the target metal, present as metal salts, stabilized nanoparticles, or combinations of the two.
These approaches are discussed in greater detail below.

===== [p182-182] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > Light-activated enzymatic crosslinking reaction (4块) =====
--[p182]-- When the critical lysine residue is caged with an o-nitrobenzyl functional group, FXIIIa is unable to crosslink the two independent peptides. After cage photolysis, however, the two peptides are rapidly joined through FXIIIa-mediated chemoenzymatic ligation. These approaches have been utilized to pattern short peptides and synthetic proteins into hydrogels and used to guide cellular growth within a material.
--[p182]-- Light-activated enzymatic crosslinking reaction
A strategy for light-activated enzymatic crosslinking utilizing factor XIII (FXIIIa) has been recently introduced by both the Lutolf and Segura groups [104, 105]. FXIIIa represents a transglutaminase enzyme that catalyzes covalent amide bond formation between the �-amino group on a lysine (K) and the �-carboxamide residue of glutamine (Q) of FKG and NQEQVSPL peptides.
--[p182]-- Aryl azides, diazirines, and benzophenones represent the most common functionalities for nonspecific photoconjugation and are highlighted in Figure 8.5.
--[p182]-- Nonspecific Photoconjugation Several photoreactive groups have been developed that couple nonspecifically with target molecules upon activation with UV light exposure. The indiscriminant nature by which these moieties couple to local molecules enables rapid addition as well as covalent linkage between biomacromolecules that are in close proximity in 3D space.

===== [p220-220] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > e-Beam Evaporation (2块) =====
--[p220]-- e-Beam evaporation was then used to deposit a 10-nm-thick layer of titanium, which acted as an adhesion layer, followed by e-beam evaporation of a 100-nm layer of gold, which served as the conductive element of the circuit.
--[p220]-- e-Beam Evaporation
e-Beam evaporation is a sputtering technique in which an electron beam is used to deposit metal onto a target under high vacuum [85]. This technique is more complicated than TE, but the issue of high temperatures of the sputtered metal is avoided. Abaddi et al. used e-beam evaporation in conjunction with MPL to fabricate a conductive circuit [10]. The structural form of the circuit was created by MPL using the commercial positive-tone photoresist AZ 5214.

===== [p217-217] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Tin Dioxide (2块) =====
--[p217]-- Tin Dioxide
Structures of tin dioxide $( \mathrm { { S n O } } _ { 2 } )$ have properties that are sensitive to the environment and can therefore be used as gas sensors. In one report, a unique metal oxide–organic composite was prepared by mixing and chelating tin(II) dichloride with acetylacetone [80]. The complex is soluble in organic solvents, and forms a gel at high weight loading, but decomposes to an organic-insoluble chelate when exposed to UV light or multiphoton absorption.
--[p217]-- This material and structures created with it by MPL can be calcined to form crystalline $\mathrm { S n O } _ { 2 } .$ . Nanowires of $\mathrm { S n O } _ { 2 }$ fabricated with this composite exhibited electric resistance that was sensitive to the relative humidity of air (Figure 9.9) [80].

===== [p123-123] Photoinitiators for Multiphoton Absorption Lithography > 6.1.1 (3块) =====
--[p123]-- 6.1.1
Multiphoton Absorption Lithography
In the past decade, three-dimensional (3D) multiphoton absorption lithography (MAL) technology based on two-photon photopolymerization (TPP) has been attracting much interest owing to its various applications to microelectromechanical systems (MEMS) [1], 3D optical devices [2, 3], lab on chip [4], and so on [5, 6].
--[p123]-- TPP is considered one of the most important applications of two-photon absorption (TPA), which contributes to the intrinsic 3D fabrication capability and the high penetration depth. The commercial photoresist resins using conventional UV active initiators have commonly been utilized in this field. These conventional initiators usually have low TPA cross sections (�), which results in low two-photon sensitivity.
--[p123]-- Therefore, large excitation power and long exposure time are needed to polymerize the resin, resulting in potential damage to the micro/nanostructures. From this perspective, the disadvantages of the conventional photoinitiators limit the versatility of 3D MAL and its scope for widespread application. Developing high-sensitivity initiators is essential to make the process more reliable and the structures to be patterned rapidly [7].

===== [p180-180] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > Photo-inducible copper-catalyzed azide-alkyne cycloaddition (pCuAAC) (3块) =====
--[p180]-- When intact, these compounds serve to block the reactivity of two species through physical masking. Upon photolysis, the reactive functional group is liberated, thereby permitting species reaction and photoconjugation to occur. To date, photocage-based regulation of the strain-promoted azide–alkyne cycloadditon (SPAAC), oxime ligation, Michael-type addition, and enzymatic crosslinking have proven the most promising for biological studies, and they are shown in Figure 8.4.
--[p180]-- In recent work by the Bowman group, photogenerated initiator fragments were shown to reduce Cu(II) to Cu(I) transiently, thereby providing photochemical control over the copper-catalyzed azide–alkyne cycloaddition (CuAAC) [87, 88]. This reaction has been utilized for photopolymerization/functionalization of hydrogel materials.
Photocage-Mediated Photoconjugation Photocages function as molecular gatekeepers for the regulation of chemical reactions.
--[p180]-- Photo-inducible copper-catalyzed azide-alkyne cycloaddition (pCuAAC)
The azide–alkyne Huisgen cycloaddition represents a 1,3-dipolar cycloaddition between an azide and a terminal or internal alkyne to give a triazole linkage. Though this reaction can be performed in the absence of a catalyst at high temperatures, pressures, or through the utilization of strained alkynes, the reaction kinetics can be increased dramatically through catalysis with Cu(I) (by a factor of ∼10<sup>7</sup>) [84–86].

===== [p170-170] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > 8.2 (1块) =====
--[p170]-- 8.2

===== [p230-230] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Structures Functionalized with Nanoparticles (3块) =====
--[p230]-- Fabrication parameters of the PCs and size distribution of the NPs can be altered to obtain targeted embedded NP emission wavelengths at or near the bandedge of the PCs [128]. NP-based microstructures can be formed by depositing the NPs onto a polymeric structure created by MPL, or performing MPL using a composite that contains the NPs or an NP precursor.
--[p230]-- Semiconductor NPs can have high quantum eficiency and narrow, tunable emission lines, which makes them well suited for micro and nanophotonic devices [127]. NPs can also be used to engineer linear or nonlinear optical properties and to increase quantum eficiency in certain wavelength ranges. The refractive index, absorption and emission wavelengths, and stop gaps of the hybrid material structure can be tuned via the composition and structure of the NPs.
--[p230]-- Structures Functionalized with Nanoparticles
The most commonly employed NPs consist of lead sulfide, lead selenide, or cadmium sulfide. Core/shell compositions are commonly used because the radiative properties ofcore-only NPs can be adversely afected by oxidation, contamination, and photobleaching [126]. By having a shell of a wider bandgap semiconductor, the quantum yield of the emitter is increased and stability is improved.

===== [p220-220] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Magnetron Sputtering (2块) =====
--[p220]-- Magnetron Sputtering
MS can generate high-quality films that can outperform other deposition techniques in terms of smoothness and metal grain uniformity [86]. Additionally, as MS involves low vacuum, some conformal deposition can be achieved because the metal vapor collides with other gaseous atoms and molecules during deposition. Previously discussed techniques require high vacuum, so these collisions are much more infrequent.
--[p220]-- MS was used by Xiong and coworkers to coat fabricated structures with a 35-nm layer of gold [87, 88]. In this work, various optical functional devices were fabricated using the commercially available MPL photoresist IP-L (Nanoscribe). Polarization-dependent [88] and wavelength-dependent [87] absorbers were fabricated and coated on metals to increase the structures’ refractive index contrast, increasing the eficiency of the device (Figure 9.10).

===== [p293-293] Fabricating Nano and Microstructures Made by Narrow Bandgap Semiconductors and Metals using Multiphoton Lithography > Further Increase the Photosensitivity with an Electron Donor (5块) =====
--[p293]-- As the laser power is increased from 0.05 to 0.4 mW, the feature size increased from sub-100 nm up to around 400 nm, which approached the size of the difraction-limited focal spot at 505 nm. In addition, the laser energy for the fabrication threshold was reduced to 0.25 μJ, which can also weaken the thermal efect to produce small silver dots.
--[p293]-- 0 5 4 \times 1 0 ^ { - 1 2 } { \mathrm { g } } { \mu } { \mathrm { J } } ^ { - 1 }$ under the exposure of the laser power of 0.1 mW, which could contribute to a larger $K _ { \mathrm { e f f } } ,$ as shown in Figure 11.8a,b. Meanwhile, the feature size of the silver dot dramatically increased with increasing the laser power above the threshold. This could be explained by the increased chemical reactivity and photoreduction sensitivity due to the photoabsorption of the reduced silver [26].
--[p293]-- Figure 11.4a shows the SEM images of silver dots fabricated under the exposure of the laser power from 0.05 mW (equivalent to the laser intensity of 2.23 GW cm<sup>–2</sup>, given the laser spot size of 505 nm) to 0.4 mW (equivalent to the laser intensity of 17.84 GW cm<sup>–2</sup>) with various exposure times between 5 and 50 ms by using the 580 nm laser beam. It can be observed that the photosensitivity of the material improved to $0 .
--[p293]-- With the exposure time of 5 ms and a laser power of 0.05 mW, we achieved the minimum silver dot of 22 nm (Figure 11.8c), equivalent to /26 of the wavelength of the laser beam.
--[p293]-- Further Increase the Photosensitivity with an Electron Donor
For further increasing the photosensitivity, we have added a water-soluble dye (2-hydroxy-4<sup>′</sup>-(2-hydroxyethoxy)-2-methylpropiophenone, 0.6 wt%) as an electron donor, which has the absorption band up to 380 nm.

===== [p215-215] Multiphoton Processing of Composite Materials and Functionalization of 3D Structures > Zinc Oxide (2块) =====
--[p215]-- The composites containing ZnO fluoresced at 570 nm. Other emission/absorption bands were obtained with rod-shaped nanoparticles. Threediensional structures based on these composites exhibited enhanced fluorescence at ∼450 nm [57].
--[p215]-- Zinc Oxide
Zinc oxide (ZnO) is a semiconductor that can give enhanced conductivity, photoemission, or hardness when added to composite structures. ZnO nanowires with a length of 1–3 μm were prepared using a hydrothermal technique and directly added to acrylate monomers up to 5 wt% [56, 57].

===== [p178-178] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > 8.4.3.2 Photochemical Polymerization and Degradation (3块) =====
--[p178]-- 8.4.3.2 Photochemical Polymerization and Degradation
Hundreds of photo-mediated reactions have been utilized for both the formation and degradation of polymers. In this section, we seek to highlight the diverse subset of these chemistries that have been performed within a biological context. Additive photoreactions are classified according to their utilization of PIs, photocaged reactive groups, or nonspecific radicals.
--[p178]-- Strategies that employ photolabile linkers to achieve subtractive degradation, as well as those ofering reversible photofunctionalization, are also described. All discussed reactions are illustrated in Figures 8.3–8.7.
Initiator-Mediated Photopolymerization To date, the majority of photopolymerization reactions are initiator-mediated.
--[p178]-- As PI identity can be selected independently from that of the reactive species, reactions utilizing PIs ofer increased tunability over initiation wavelengths, solvent composition, and reaction kinetics. Some common PI-mediated polymerization mechanisms that have been performed for biomaterial synthesis include vinyl chain polymerization, thiol–ene and thiol–yne reactions, and the photo-inducible copper-catalyzed azide–alkyne cycloaddition (pCuAAC) (shown in Figure 8.3).

===== [p183-183] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > Diazirine (2块) =====
--[p183]-- These molecules have been useful in studying the efects of protein interactions in cells [112, 113], crosslinking protein-based hydrogels [114], and identifying targets of membrane receptors [115, 116].
--[p183]-- Diazirine
In the presence of UV light (330 < � < 370 nm), diazirine compounds undergo photolysis to form a highly reactive carbine intermediate while simultaneously giving of nitrogen gas [111]. The resulting carbine yields eficient insertion into hydrogen–carbon and hydrogen–nitrogen bonds, as well as with unsaturated bonds within target molecules.

===== [p182-183] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > Aryl azides (2块) =====
--[p182]-- Aryl azides
Perhaps the most popular of the nonspecific photoconjugation reactions, aryl azides form short-lived nitrenes upon exposure to UV light (250 < � < 350 nm) [106]. These intermediate nitrenes can either insert nonspecifically into carbon–hydrogen bonds, or rearrange to form ring-expanded dehydroazepines prior to addition reaction with nucleophiles or carbon–hydrogen bonds. Photocrosslinking of aryl azides has
Nonspecific
--[p183]-- been extremely beneficial in capturing and probing protein interactions [107, 108], patterning cell-adhesive proteins to surfaces [109], and identifying peptide–receptor binding domains [110].

===== [p84-84] Modeling of Polymerization Processes > Conclusion (1块) =====
--[p84]-- Conclusion
In this chapter, we studied the physical phenomena that, in our opinion, have a major efect on the laser nanopolymerization process, concerning the limiting capabilities of MPP. They are the difusion of molecules and fluctuations in polymer nanofeatures. By solving model problems, we demonstrated the approaches that can be employed for the calculations concerning the realistic polymerizable resins in writing experiment and technology.

===== [p161-161] Hybrid Materials for Multiphoton Polymerization > Conclusions (1块) =====
--[p161]-- Conclusions
The potential of photostructurable hybrid materials for MPP fabrication has started to emerge. Their mechanical and chemical stability, combined with their superior MPP structuring ability and functionalization potential, makes them ideal candidates for a wide range of applications including photonics, metamaterials, and biomedical implants.

===== [p39-39] 2 Characterization of 2PA Chromophores > Indirect Methods (1块) =====
--[p39]-- Indirect Methods
By “indirect” we mean that a material is excited optically to be sensed by some other means than the transmission of the excitation beam itself. These “other means” could include measuring the transmittance of a probe, heating of materials, fluorescence, and so on. For example, an excitation beam induces excited states via 2PA whose presence is sensed by their fluorescence in which the intensity is proportional to how many excited states were produced via 2PA.
We give examples of these indirect methods in the following.

===== [p182-182] Photopolymers for Multiphoton Lithography in Biomaterials and Hydrogels > Michael-type addition (2块) =====
--[p182]-- The Shoichet group has demonstrated that thiols can be masked with both 2-nitrobenzyl- and coumarin-based photocages, and will subsequently react with maleimides upon irradiation [99–101]. Moreover, the Bowman group has demonstrated that NPPOC can serve as a photolabile protecting group for primary amines, which enables thiol-Michael additions with acrylates to be phototriggered [102].
These chemistries have been used to form hydrogel biomaterials [103] as well as to guide 3D cell function through immobilized biomolecules [99, 101].
--[p182]-- Michael-type addition
The Michael-type addition ofnucleophiles to electron-deficient alkenes represents one of the most common reactions in organic chemistry. Though a variety of nucleophilic Michael donors/acceptors exist, thiol–maleimide and amine–acrylate represent important variants, both of which have been controlled with light.

===== [p54-54] 2 Characterization of 2PA Chromophores > Conclusion (4块) =====
--[p54]-- However, the magnitudes of the nonlinearities are still not as large as is needed for many of the applications including all-optical switching and optical limiting.
--[p54]-- The variety of nonlinear processes occurring in diferent materials are rich and complicated, and it is rarely the case that a single measurement at a single wavelength with a single pulsewidth can determine the physics involved in a nonlinear interaction. Modern nonlinear spectroscopic techniques are essential. Ultrafast nonlinearities often require femtosecond resolution to unambiguously determine their sign and magnitude.
--[p54]-- Longer pulses display other efects, such as ESA and refraction, which can even dominate the nonlinear response. The nonlinearities occurring in organic materials mimic the nonlinearities in semiconductors, where free-carrier efects take the place of excited-state efects. However, unlike in semiconductors where the band structure determines the response, the individual molecular levels in organics can be engineered, allowing the nonlinear response to be altered.
--[p54]-- Conclusion
There are many diferent experimental methodologies for measuring NLA. The associated NLR can often lead to problems of unambiguously determining the nonlinear loss; thus, it can be useful to simultaneously determine the NLR. This can also be valuable for determining the underlying physical process or processes that can often be dificult. Parametric studies of the wavelength dependence, temporal dependence, and sometimes the optical geometry dependence can also be important.
